Like if I’m with my sister or a friend, I’m not talking about work the whole time, but it’s still in my head because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And then those little comments on the radio, they don’t just disappear when I clock out.\n\nMy aunt always says I need to “leave work at work,” which is funny because she used to bring home stacks of papers from her job, so I don’t know why she says that. But anyway… when I’m around family, I still feel like certain drivers are reporting on me. People say it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell it’s more than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have a day where you don't think about people reporting on you at all?\n\nPatient: No… not really a whole day. Maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy and it’s quieter, like if I’m cleaning or watching something, but then it comes back.\n\nBecause I’m here for that reason, you know—dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then those little radio comments. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, like any job has that, and my old supervisor used to say the system “just logs everything,” but… I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. So even on a day off, it’s not like it’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… when I’m alone at home too. It’s not like I’m sitting there talking to anybody, but it’s still… in my head. I’ll be in my apartment, like making food or folding towels—I always fold them the way my mom taught me, corners lined up—and then I start thinking about the route changes and how dispatch and compliance see it all through the computer system.\n\nAnd those radio comments, the little ones, they kind of stay with me. People say that’s just normal oversight, like every job tracks things, and I know that’s what they say, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. Even if I’m home alone, it doesn’t really feel separate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you for reasons that have nothing to do with your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… mostly it’s about work. Like, I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio, you know? People say that’s just normal oversight, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nOutside of that… I don’t know. Sometimes it feels like it carries over, but the reason for it is still the job, the routes, the stuff they’re watching. My cousin was asking me the other day about my schedule, and she always asks too many questions anyway—she did that even when we were kids, like checking who ate the last popsicle—but I don’t think it’s some totally separate reason. It comes back to work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are doing it because they believe you've done something wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s because they think I did something wrong exactly… maybe they think I’m changing routes when I’m not supposed to, or that I’m trying to get around the system. But that’s why I keep saying dispatch and compliance are tracking the route changes through the computer system.\n\nAnd then the radio comments happen, little ones, not direct, but you can tell. Like when drivers act like they just “noticed” something—people say it’s normal oversight, every job has that, but certain drivers are reporting on me. I mean, my aunt used to say if people are watching you, it’s because they’re looking for a reason, and she’d say that about neighbors too, she was always like that with the blinds half open… but with this, it’s more about work. So yeah, maybe they think I’ve done something wrong, or they’re trying to make it look that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reasons certain drivers are reporting on you make sense to you, or do they seem strange or unbelievable?\n\nPatient: I mean… it makes sense to me in the moment, because the route changes are right there in the computer system, and dispatch and compliance can see that stuff. So if drivers are making little comments on the radio after that, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nBut I know other people say, “That’s just normal oversight, everybody gets tracked at work,” and maybe that’s what it looks like to them. I don’t know. My old supervisor used to say if you’re five minutes off schedule, everybody acts like the whole day is ruined, and then people start talking.\n\nSo… strange? Maybe to other people. To me it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me, and the radio comments kind of prove it, even if nobody says it straight out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're misunderstanding the situation and that nobody is actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah… like, people keep saying maybe I’m taking it the wrong way, that it’s just normal tracking and oversight. And I try to tell myself that sometimes.\n\nBut then I’m back at work and dispatch and compliance are watching the route changes in the computer system, and then I hear those little comments on the radio, and it lines up too much. It’s like when my mom used to say, “If it happens once, fine, but if it keeps happening, pay attention,” and that kind of sticks in my head.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’m just misunderstanding it. Maybe I don’t have every piece of it, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that certain drivers are actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have moments where I try to doubt it. Like when people say, “It’s just normal oversight, everybody’s route changes are in the computer,” I’ll think maybe I’m making too much of it.\n\nBut then I hear the little comments on the radio again, and it’s like… no, that wasn’t nothing. Certain drivers know things they wouldn’t know unless they were reporting or checking. And dispatch and compliance, they’ve got the computer system right there.\n\nSo I can doubt it for a minute, maybe, but it doesn’t really last.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could convince you that certain drivers are not reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe if dispatch showed me everything, like the computer records and who can see the route changes, maybe that would help for a little while.\n\nBut even then, it’s the radio comments that get me. Because people can say, “No one’s reporting on you, it’s just oversight,” and then I’ll hear a driver say some little thing that lines up with where I was or what changed, and it feels like… okay, so how would they know that?\n\nIt’s like when you lose your keys and everyone says, “They’re probably in your bag,” but you already checked your bag three times, and then—sorry, that’s not the same thing exactly. I just mean people can tell you something, but if what you’re seeing doesn’t match it, it’s hard to believe.\n\nSo I guess there could be evidence, but I don’t know what would really convince me unless the comments stopped too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be wrong about certain drivers reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Possible… I mean, I guess anything is possible. People say that to me a lot, like “maybe you’re reading into it,” and I try to sit with that.\n\nBut then I come back to the same thing: dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio. Like someone says something small, but it fits too closely with where I was or what changed. And certain drivers… they just know things.\n\nIt reminds me of when my aunt used to say if two people know a secret, it’s not really a secret anymore—sorry, I know that’s kind of off. I just mean information gets around. So maybe I could be wrong in some tiny piece of it, but I don’t think I’m wrong that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: If dispatch and compliance showed you proof that no drivers are reporting on you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to believe them, I really would. If they sat me down and showed me the computer system, like who can see the route changes and what gets logged, maybe I’d feel calmer for a minute.\n\nBut I don’t know if proof from dispatch and compliance would be enough, because they’re part of it, or at least they’re the ones with the system. And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

But that’s why I keep saying dispatch and compliance are tracking the route changes through the computer system.\n\nAnd then the radio comments happen, little ones, not direct, but you can tell. Like when drivers act like they just “noticed” something—people say it’s normal oversight, every job has that, but certain drivers are reporting on me. I mean, my aunt used to say if people are watching you, it’s because they’re looking for a reason, and she’d say that about neighbors too, she was always like that with the blinds half open… but with this, it’s more about work. So yeah, maybe they think I’ve done something wrong, or they’re trying to make it look that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reasons certain drivers are reporting on you make sense to you, or do they seem strange or unbelievable?\n\nPatient: I mean… it makes sense to me in the moment, because the route changes are right there in the computer system, and dispatch and compliance can see that stuff. So if drivers are making little comments on the radio after that, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nBut I know other people say, “That’s just normal oversight, everybody gets tracked at work,” and maybe that’s what it looks like to them. I don’t know. My old supervisor used to say if you’re five minutes off schedule, everybody acts like the whole day is ruined, and then people start talking.\n\nSo… strange? Maybe to other people. To me it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me, and the radio comments kind of prove it, even if nobody says it straight out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're misunderstanding the situation and that nobody is actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah… like, people keep saying maybe I’m taking it the wrong way, that it’s just normal tracking and oversight. And I try to tell myself that sometimes.\n\nBut then I’m back at work and dispatch and compliance are watching the route changes in the computer system, and then I hear those little comments on the radio, and it lines up too much. It’s like when my mom used to say, “If it happens once, fine, but if it keeps happening, pay attention,” and that kind of sticks in my head.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’m just misunderstanding it. Maybe I don’t have every piece of it, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that certain drivers are actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have moments where I try to doubt it. Like when people say, “It’s just normal oversight, everybody’s route changes are in the computer,” I’ll think maybe I’m making too much of it.\n\nBut then I hear the little comments on the radio again, and it’s like… no, that wasn’t nothing. Certain drivers know things they wouldn’t know unless they were reporting or checking. And dispatch and compliance, they’ve got the computer system right there.\n\nSo I can doubt it for a minute, maybe, but it doesn’t really last.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could convince you that certain drivers are not reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe if dispatch showed me everything, like the computer records and who can see the route changes, maybe that would help for a little while.\n\nBut even then, it’s the radio comments that get me. Because people can say, “No one’s reporting on you, it’s just oversight,” and then I’ll hear a driver say some little thing that lines up with where I was or what changed, and it feels like… okay, so how would they know that?\n\nIt’s like when you lose your keys and everyone says, “They’re probably in your bag,” but you already checked your bag three times, and then—sorry, that’s not the same thing exactly. I just mean people can tell you something, but if what you’re seeing doesn’t match it, it’s hard to believe.\n\nSo I guess there could be evidence, but I don’t know what would really convince me unless the comments stopped too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be wrong about certain drivers reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Possible… I mean, I guess anything is possible. People say that to me a lot, like “maybe you’re reading into it,” and I try to sit with that.\n\nBut then I come back to the same thing: dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio. Like someone says something small, but it fits too closely with where I was or what changed. And certain drivers… they just know things.\n\nIt reminds me of when my aunt used to say if two people know a secret, it’s not really a secret anymore—sorry, I know that’s kind of off. I just mean information gets around. So maybe I could be wrong in some tiny piece of it, but I don’t think I’m wrong that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: If dispatch and compliance showed you proof that no drivers are reporting on you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to believe them, I really would. If they sat me down and showed me the computer system, like who can see the route changes and what gets logged, maybe I’d feel calmer for a minute.\n\nBut I don’t know if proof from dispatch and compliance would be enough, because they’re part of it, or at least they’re the ones with the system. And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that certain drivers are reporting on you is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that, and I guess I know what you’re getting at. Like, am I filling in the blanks myself. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that certain drivers are reporting on you is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that, and I guess I know what you’re getting at. Like, am I filling in the blanks myself. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think that your belief about certain drivers reporting on you could be a mistaken idea that is not based in reality?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I guess it could be mistaken, in the way anybody can misread something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. But that’s hard for me to accept because I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with it.

People say, “That’s just normal oversight,” and I get that trucking has systems and logs and all that. My uncle used to say every job has somebody watching the numbers—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, it doesn’t feel random to me. Certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t, and it feels like they’re reporting on me.

So… maybe there’s a chance I’m wrong, but I don’t really believe it’s not based in reality.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been… off. Dispatch and compliance keep tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there are these little comments over the radio that line up too much to be random. People say it’s just normal oversight, like, “That’s their job,” but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nAnd I mean, I’ve worked transportation long enough to know what normal check-ins sound like. It’s not like when the schedule gets messed up because somebody double-parks near the depot or when the tablet freezes—I know those things happen. This feels different. Like they’re watching what I do and talking around it, not directly to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about outside of work exactly… I mean, it mostly happens through dispatch and compliance, the route changes, the computer system, the radio comments. That’s where I notice it.\n\nBut it’s like once certain drivers start reporting on you, you don’t really know where it stops, you know? Like, I’ll be thinking about it even after my shift, because the way they say things over the radio—little things—lines up too much. And people keep saying it’s just normal oversight, but I’ve been doing this long enough to know the difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are reporting on you when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it’s mostly work, like dispatch and compliance with the route changes and the computer system, and those radio comments. That’s where it starts.\n\nBut even when I’m not trying to think about work, I still know certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s hard to just turn that off. People act like it’s normal oversight, but I’ve been there long enough to know when something’s being said around me. Like, not straight out, but little things line up. And then I’ll be home trying to make dinner or whatever, and I’ll remember what somebody said over the radio and it just… it doesn’t feel random.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about people reporting on you every day, even on your days off?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much. Even on my days off it’s there, because I know dispatch and compliance have been tracking the route changes through the computer system, and then those little radio comments happen and it sticks with me.\n\nLike, I’ll be off work and trying to just do laundry or go to the store, and I’ll think about how certain drivers report things back. People keep saying it’s just oversight, like that’s normal, and I get that jobs have systems—my cousin used to say every workplace has paperwork for everything—but this is different. I can tell when comments are aimed around me, even if nobody says my name straight out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you when you're with friends or family, outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, I guess. Like if I’m with my sister or a friend, I’m not talking about work the whole time, but it’s still in my head because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And then those little comments on the radio, they don’t just disappear when I clock out.\n\nMy aunt always says I need to “leave work at work,” which is funny because she used to bring home stacks of papers from her job, so I don’t know why she says that. But anyway… when I’m around family, I still feel like certain drivers are reporting on me. People say it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell it’s more than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have a day where you don't think about people reporting on you at all?\n\nPatient: No… not really a whole day. Maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy and it’s quieter, like if I’m cleaning or watching something, but then it comes back.\n\nBecause I’m here for that reason, you know—dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then those little radio comments. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, like any job has that, and my old supervisor used to say the system “just logs everything,” but… I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. So even on a day off, it’s not like it’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… when I’m alone at home too. It’s not like I’m sitting there talking to anybody, but it’s still… in my head. I’ll be in my apartment, like making food or folding towels—I always fold them the way my mom taught me, corners lined up—and then I start thinking about the route changes and how dispatch and compliance see it all through the computer system.\n\nAnd those radio comments, the little ones, they kind of stay with me. People say that’s just normal oversight, like every job tracks things, and I know that’s what they say, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. Even if I’m home alone, it doesn’t really feel separate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you for reasons that have nothing to do with your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… mostly it’s about work. Like, I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio, you know? People say that’s just normal oversight, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nOutside of that… I don’t know. Sometimes it feels like it carries over, but the reason for it is still the job, the routes, the stuff they’re watching. My cousin was asking me the other day about my schedule, and she always asks too many questions anyway—she did that even when we were kids, like checking who ate the last popsicle—but I don’t think it’s some totally separate reason. It comes back to work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are doing it because they believe you've done something wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s because they think I did something wrong exactly… maybe they think I’m changing routes when I’m not supposed to, or that I’m trying to get around the system. But that’s why I keep saying dispatch and compliance are tracking the route changes through the computer system.\n\nAnd then the radio comments happen, little ones, not direct, but you can tell. Like when drivers act like they just “noticed” something—people say it’s normal oversight, every job has that, but certain drivers are reporting on me. I mean, my aunt used to say if people are watching you, it’s because they’re looking for a reason, and she’d say that about neighbors too, she was always like that with the blinds half open… but with this, it’s more about work. So yeah, maybe they think I’ve done something wrong, or they’re trying to make it look that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reasons certain drivers are reporting on you make sense to you, or do they seem strange or unbelievable?\n\nPatient: I mean… it makes sense to me in the moment, because the route changes are right there in the computer system, and dispatch and compliance can see that stuff. So if drivers are making little comments on the radio after that, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nBut I know other people say, “That’s just normal oversight, everybody gets tracked at work,” and maybe that’s what it looks like to them. I don’t know. My old supervisor used to say if you’re five minutes off schedule, everybody acts like the whole day is ruined, and then people start talking.\n\nSo… strange? Maybe to other people. To me it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me, and the radio comments kind of prove it, even if nobody says it straight out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're misunderstanding the situation and that nobody is actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah… like, people keep saying maybe I’m taking it the wrong way, that it’s just normal tracking and oversight. And I try to tell myself that sometimes.\n\nBut then I’m back at work and dispatch and compliance are watching the route changes in the computer system, and then I hear those little comments on the radio, and it lines up too much. It’s like when my mom used to say, “If it happens once, fine, but if it keeps happening, pay attention,” and that kind of sticks in my head.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’m just misunderstanding it. Maybe I don’t have every piece of it, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that certain drivers are actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have moments where I try to doubt it. Like when people say, “It’s just normal oversight, everybody’s route changes are in the computer,” I’ll think maybe I’m making too much of it.\n\nBut then I hear the little comments on the radio again, and it’s like… no, that wasn’t nothing. Certain drivers know things they wouldn’t know unless they were reporting or checking. And dispatch and compliance, they’ve got the computer system right there.\n\nSo I can doubt it for a minute, maybe, but it doesn’t really last.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could convince you that certain drivers are not reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe if dispatch showed me everything, like the computer records and who can see the route changes, maybe that would help for a little while.\n\nBut even then, it’s the radio comments that get me. Because people can say, “No one’s reporting on you, it’s just oversight,” and then I’ll hear a driver say some little thing that lines up with where I was or what changed, and it feels like… okay, so how would they know that?\n\nIt’s like when you lose your keys and everyone says, “They’re probably in your bag,” but you already checked your bag three times, and then—sorry, that’s not the same thing exactly. I just mean people can tell you something, but if what you’re seeing doesn’t match it, it’s hard to believe.\n\nSo I guess there could be evidence, but I don’t know what would really convince me unless the comments stopped too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be wrong about certain drivers reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Possible… I mean, I guess anything is possible. People say that to me a lot, like “maybe you’re reading into it,” and I try to sit with that.\n\nBut then I come back to the same thing: dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio. Like someone says something small, but it fits too closely with where I was or what changed. And certain drivers… they just know things.\n\nIt reminds me of when my aunt used to say if two people know a secret, it’s not really a secret anymore—sorry, I know that’s kind of off. I just mean information gets around. So maybe I could be wrong in some tiny piece of it, but I don’t think I’m wrong that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: If dispatch and compliance showed you proof that no drivers are reporting on you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to believe them, I really would. If they sat me down and showed me the computer system, like who can see the route changes and what gets logged, maybe I’d feel calmer for a minute.\n\nBut I don’t know if proof from dispatch and compliance would be enough, because they’re part of it, or at least they’re the ones with the system. And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that certain drivers are reporting on you is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that, and I guess I know what you’re getting at. Like, am I filling in the blanks myself. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about certain drivers reporting on you could be a mistaken idea that is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could be mistaken, in the way anybody can misread something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. But that’s hard for me to accept because I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with it.\n\nPeople say, “That’s just normal oversight,” and I get that trucking has systems and logs and all that. My uncle used to say every job has somebody watching the numbers—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, it doesn’t feel random to me. Certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t, and it feels like they’re reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe there’s a chance I’m wrong, but I don’t really believe it’s not based in reality.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like when people say, “It’s just normal oversight, everybody’s route changes are in the computer,” I’ll think maybe I’m making too much of it.\n\nBut then I hear the little comments on the radio again, and it’s like… no, that wasn’t nothing. Certain drivers know things they wouldn’t know unless they were reporting or checking. And dispatch and compliance, they’ve got the computer system right there.\n\nSo I can doubt it for a minute, maybe, but it doesn’t really last.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could convince you that certain drivers are not reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe if dispatch showed me everything, like the computer records and who can see the route changes, maybe that would help for a little while.\n\nBut even then, it’s the radio comments that get me. Because people can say, “No one’s reporting on you, it’s just oversight,” and then I’ll hear a driver say some little thing that lines up with where I was or what changed, and it feels like… okay, so how would they know that?\n\nIt’s like when you lose your keys and everyone says, “They’re probably in your bag,” but you already checked your bag three times, and then—sorry, that’s not the same thing exactly. I just mean people can tell you something, but if what you’re seeing doesn’t match it, it’s hard to believe.\n\nSo I guess there could be evidence, but I don’t know what would really convince me unless the comments stopped too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be wrong about certain drivers reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Possible… I mean, I guess anything is possible. People say that to me a lot, like “maybe you’re reading into it,” and I try to sit with that.\n\nBut then I come back to the same thing: dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio. Like someone says something small, but it fits too closely with where I was or what changed. And certain drivers… they just know things.\n\nIt reminds me of when my aunt used to say if two people know a secret, it’s not really a secret anymore—sorry, I know that’s kind of off. I just mean information gets around. So maybe I could be wrong in some tiny piece of it, but I don’t think I’m wrong that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: If dispatch and compliance showed you proof that no drivers are reporting on you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to believe them, I really would. If they sat me down and showed me the computer system, like who can see the route changes and what gets logged, maybe I’d feel calmer for a minute.\n\nBut I don’t know if proof from dispatch and compliance would be enough, because they’re part of it, or at least they’re the ones with the system. And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that certain drivers are reporting on you is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that, and I guess I know what you’re getting at. Like, am I filling in the blanks myself. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about certain drivers reporting on you could be a mistaken idea that is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could be mistaken, in the way anybody can misread something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. But that’s hard for me to accept because I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with it.\n\nPeople say, “That’s just normal oversight,” and I get that trucking has systems and logs and all that. My uncle used to say every job has somebody watching the numbers—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, it doesn’t feel random to me. Certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t, and it feels like they’re reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe there’s a chance I’m wrong, but I don’t really believe it’s not based in reality.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… before all this, I wasn’t thinking about certain drivers reporting on me, no. I just did my routes and figured dispatch was doing normal dispatch stuff.

But once the route changes started getting tracked through the computer system, and then I’d hear those little comments on the radio that matched what had happened, it was hard to go back to thinking it was nothing. People say it’s just normal oversight, and I know every job has that—like even my friend at the bus company says they watch everything there too—but this feels more specific than that.

So… there probably was a time I didn’t believe it. But now I can’t really unsee it. I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.

Like if I’m with my sister or a friend, I’m not talking about work the whole time, but it’s still in my head because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system. Sometimes it feels like it carries over, but the reason for it is still the job, the routes, the stuff they’re watching. My cousin was asking me the other day about my schedule, and she always asks too many questions anyway—she did that even when we were kids, like checking who ate the last popsicle—but I don’t think it’s some totally separate reason. It comes back to work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are doing it because they believe you've done something wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s because they think I did something wrong exactly… maybe they think I’m changing routes when I’m not supposed to, or that I’m trying to get around the system. But that’s why I keep saying dispatch and compliance are tracking the route changes through the computer system.\n\nAnd then the radio comments happen, little ones, not direct, but you can tell. Like when drivers act like they just “noticed” something—people say it’s normal oversight, every job has that, but certain drivers are reporting on me. I mean, my aunt used to say if people are watching you, it’s because they’re looking for a reason, and she’d say that about neighbors too, she was always like that with the blinds half open… but with this, it’s more about work. So yeah, maybe they think I’ve done something wrong, or they’re trying to make it look that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reasons certain drivers are reporting on you make sense to you, or do they seem strange or unbelievable?\n\nPatient: I mean… it makes sense to me in the moment, because the route changes are right there in the computer system, and dispatch and compliance can see that stuff. So if drivers are making little comments on the radio after that, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nBut I know other people say, “That’s just normal oversight, everybody gets tracked at work,” and maybe that’s what it looks like to them. I don’t know. My old supervisor used to say if you’re five minutes off schedule, everybody acts like the whole day is ruined, and then people start talking.\n\nSo… strange? Maybe to other people. To me it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me, and the radio comments kind of prove it, even if nobody says it straight out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're misunderstanding the situation and that nobody is actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah… like, people keep saying maybe I’m taking it the wrong way, that it’s just normal tracking and oversight. And I try to tell myself that sometimes.\n\nBut then I’m back at work and dispatch and compliance are watching the route changes in the computer system, and then I hear those little comments on the radio, and it lines up too much. It’s like when my mom used to say, “If it happens once, fine, but if it keeps happening, pay attention,” and that kind of sticks in my head.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’m just misunderstanding it. Maybe I don’t have every piece of it, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that certain drivers are actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have moments where I try to doubt it. Like when people say, “It’s just normal oversight, everybody’s route changes are in the computer,” I’ll think maybe I’m making too much of it.\n\nBut then I hear the little comments on the radio again, and it’s like… no, that wasn’t nothing. Certain drivers know things they wouldn’t know unless they were reporting or checking. And dispatch and compliance, they’ve got the computer system right there.\n\nSo I can doubt it for a minute, maybe, but it doesn’t really last.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could convince you that certain drivers are not reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe if dispatch showed me everything, like the computer records and who can see the route changes, maybe that would help for a little while.\n\nBut even then, it’s the radio comments that get me. Because people can say, “No one’s reporting on you, it’s just oversight,” and then I’ll hear a driver say some little thing that lines up with where I was or what changed, and it feels like… okay, so how would they know that?\n\nIt’s like when you lose your keys and everyone says, “They’re probably in your bag,” but you already checked your bag three times, and then—sorry, that’s not the same thing exactly. I just mean people can tell you something, but if what you’re seeing doesn’t match it, it’s hard to believe.\n\nSo I guess there could be evidence, but I don’t know what would really convince me unless the comments stopped too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be wrong about certain drivers reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Possible… I mean, I guess anything is possible. People say that to me a lot, like “maybe you’re reading into it,” and I try to sit with that.\n\nBut then I come back to the same thing: dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio. Like someone says something small, but it fits too closely with where I was or what changed. And certain drivers… they just know things.\n\nIt reminds me of when my aunt used to say if two people know a secret, it’s not really a secret anymore—sorry, I know that’s kind of off. I just mean information gets around. So maybe I could be wrong in some tiny piece of it, but I don’t think I’m wrong that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: If dispatch and compliance showed you proof that no drivers are reporting on you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to believe them, I really would. If they sat me down and showed me the computer system, like who can see the route changes and what gets logged, maybe I’d feel calmer for a minute.\n\nBut I don’t know if proof from dispatch and compliance would be enough, because they’re part of it, or at least they’re the ones with the system. And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that certain drivers are reporting on you is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that, and I guess I know what you’re getting at. Like, am I filling in the blanks myself. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about certain drivers reporting on you could be a mistaken idea that is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could be mistaken, in the way anybody can misread something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. But that’s hard for me to accept because I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with it.\n\nPeople say, “That’s just normal oversight,” and I get that trucking has systems and logs and all that. My uncle used to say every job has somebody watching the numbers—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, it doesn’t feel random to me. Certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t, and it feels like they’re reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe there’s a chance I’m wrong, but I don’t really believe it’s not based in reality.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe certain drivers were reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this, I wasn’t thinking about certain drivers reporting on me, no. I just did my routes and figured dispatch was doing normal dispatch stuff.\n\nBut once the route changes started getting tracked through the computer system, and then I’d hear those little comments on the radio that matched what had happened, it was hard to go back to thinking it was nothing. People say it’s just normal oversight, and I know every job has that—like even my friend at the bus company says they watch everything there too—but this feels more specific than that.\n\nSo… there probably was a time I didn’t believe it. But now I can’t really unsee it. I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about certain drivers reporting on you could be a mistaken idea that is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could be mistaken, in the way anybody can misread something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. But that’s hard for me to accept because I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with it.\n\nPeople say, “That’s just normal oversight,” and I get that trucking has systems and logs and all that. My uncle used to say every job has somebody watching the numbers—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, it doesn’t feel random to me. Certain drivers seem to know things they shouldn’t, and it feels like they’re reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe there’s a chance I’m wrong, but I don’t really believe it’s not based in reality.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe certain drivers were reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this, I wasn’t thinking about certain drivers reporting on me, no. I just did my routes and figured dispatch was doing normal dispatch stuff.\n\nBut once the route changes started getting tracked through the computer system, and then I’d hear those little comments on the radio that matched what had happened, it was hard to go back to thinking it was nothing. People say it’s just normal oversight, and I know every job has that—like even my friend at the bus company says they watch everything there too—but this feels more specific than that.\n\nSo… there probably was a time I didn’t believe it. But now I can’t really unsee it. I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think it's possible that the comments on the radio are just coincidental and not actually about you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… possible, I guess, like anything is possible. Radios are busy, people say things all day, and maybe some of it could just line up by accident.

But it doesn’t feel that way when dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments right after, like they’re tied to what I just did. It’s not just one comment, you know? It’s the pattern.

My old supervisor used to say people hear what they expect to hear—well, not my old supervisor, maybe it was someone in training—but anyway, I’ve thought about that. I just still come back to the same thing: even if people call it normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because things at work have been… off. Dispatch and compliance keep tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there are these little comments over the radio that line up too much to be random. People say it’s just normal oversight, like, “That’s their job,” but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nAnd I mean, I’ve worked transportation long enough to know what normal check-ins sound like. It’s not like when the schedule gets messed up because somebody double-parks near the depot or when the tablet freezes—I know those things happen. This feels different. Like they’re watching what I do and talking around it, not directly to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about outside of work exactly… I mean, it mostly happens through dispatch and compliance, the route changes, the computer system, the radio comments. That’s where I notice it.\n\nBut it’s like once certain drivers start reporting on you, you don’t really know where it stops, you know? Like, I’ll be thinking about it even after my shift, because the way they say things over the radio—little things—lines up too much. And people keep saying it’s just normal oversight, but I’ve been doing this long enough to know the difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think people are reporting on you when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it’s mostly work, like dispatch and compliance with the route changes and the computer system, and those radio comments. That’s where it starts.\n\nBut even when I’m not trying to think about work, I still know certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s hard to just turn that off. People act like it’s normal oversight, but I’ve been there long enough to know when something’s being said around me. Like, not straight out, but little things line up. And then I’ll be home trying to make dinner or whatever, and I’ll remember what somebody said over the radio and it just… it doesn’t feel random.\n\nDoctor: Do you think about people reporting on you every day, even on your days off?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much. Even on my days off it’s there, because I know dispatch and compliance have been tracking the route changes through the computer system, and then those little radio comments happen and it sticks with me.\n\nLike, I’ll be off work and trying to just do laundry or go to the store, and I’ll think about how certain drivers report things back. People keep saying it’s just oversight, like that’s normal, and I get that jobs have systems—my cousin used to say every workplace has paperwork for everything—but this is different. I can tell when comments are aimed around me, even if nobody says my name straight out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you when you're with friends or family, outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, I guess. Like if I’m with my sister or a friend, I’m not talking about work the whole time, but it’s still in my head because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And then those little comments on the radio, they don’t just disappear when I clock out.\n\nMy aunt always says I need to “leave work at work,” which is funny because she used to bring home stacks of papers from her job, so I don’t know why she says that. But anyway… when I’m around family, I still feel like certain drivers are reporting on me. People say it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell it’s more than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have a day where you don't think about people reporting on you at all?\n\nPatient: No… not really a whole day. Maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy and it’s quieter, like if I’m cleaning or watching something, but then it comes back.\n\nBecause I’m here for that reason, you know—dispatch and compliance tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then those little radio comments. People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, like any job has that, and my old supervisor used to say the system “just logs everything,” but… I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. So even on a day off, it’s not like it’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… when I’m alone at home too. It’s not like I’m sitting there talking to anybody, but it’s still… in my head. I’ll be in my apartment, like making food or folding towels—I always fold them the way my mom taught me, corners lined up—and then I start thinking about the route changes and how dispatch and compliance see it all through the computer system.\n\nAnd those radio comments, the little ones, they kind of stay with me. People say that’s just normal oversight, like every job tracks things, and I know that’s what they say, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. Even if I’m home alone, it doesn’t really feel separate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are reporting on you for reasons that have nothing to do with your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… mostly it’s about work. Like, I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio, you know? People say that’s just normal oversight, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nOutside of that… I don’t know. Sometimes it feels like it carries over, but the reason for it is still the job, the routes, the stuff they’re watching. My cousin was asking me the other day about my schedule, and she always asks too many questions anyway—she did that even when we were kids, like checking who ate the last popsicle—but I don’t think it’s some totally separate reason. It comes back to work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are doing it because they believe you've done something wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s because they think I did something wrong exactly… maybe they think I’m changing routes when I’m not supposed to, or that I’m trying to get around the system. But that’s why I keep saying dispatch and compliance are tracking the route changes through the computer system.\n\nAnd then the radio comments happen, little ones, not direct, but you can tell. Like when drivers act like they just “noticed” something—people say it’s normal oversight, every job has that, but certain drivers are reporting on me. I mean, my aunt used to say if people are watching you, it’s because they’re looking for a reason, and she’d say that about neighbors too, she was always like that with the blinds half open… but with this, it’s more about work. So yeah, maybe they think I’ve done something wrong, or they’re trying to make it look that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reasons certain drivers are reporting on you make sense to you, or do they seem strange or unbelievable?\n\nPatient: I mean… it makes sense to me in the moment, because the route changes are right there in the computer system, and dispatch and compliance can see that stuff. So if drivers are making little comments on the radio after that, it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nBut I know other people say, “That’s just normal oversight, everybody gets tracked at work,” and maybe that’s what it looks like to them. I don’t know. My old supervisor used to say if you’re five minutes off schedule, everybody acts like the whole day is ruined, and then people start talking.\n\nSo… strange? Maybe to other people. To me it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me, and the radio comments kind of prove it, even if nobody says it straight out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that maybe you're misunderstanding the situation and that nobody is actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, yeah… like, people keep saying maybe I’m taking it the wrong way, that it’s just normal tracking and oversight. And I try to tell myself that sometimes.\n\nBut then I’m back at work and dispatch and compliance are watching the route changes in the computer system, and then I hear those little comments on the radio, and it lines up too much. It’s like when my mom used to say, “If it happens once, fine, but if it keeps happening, pay attention,” and that kind of sticks in my head.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’m just misunderstanding it. Maybe I don’t have every piece of it, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that certain drivers are actually reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have moments where I try to doubt it. Like when people say, “It’s just normal oversight, everybody’s route changes are in the computer,” I’ll think maybe I’m making too much of it.\n\nBut then I hear the little comments on the radio again, and it’s like… no, that wasn’t nothing. Certain drivers know things they wouldn’t know unless they were reporting or checking. And dispatch and compliance, they’ve got the computer system right there.\n\nSo I can doubt it for a minute, maybe, but it doesn’t really last.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could convince you that certain drivers are not reporting on you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe if dispatch showed me everything, like the computer records and who can see the route changes, maybe that would help for a little while.\n\nBut even then, it’s the radio comments that get me. Because people can say, “No one’s reporting on you, it’s just oversight,” and then I’ll hear a driver say some little thing that lines up with where I was or what changed, and it feels like… okay, so how would they know that?\n\nIt’s like when you lose your keys and everyone says, “They’re probably in your bag,” but you already checked your bag three times, and then—sorry, that’s not the same thing exactly. I just mean people can tell you something, but if what you’re seeing doesn’t match it, it’s hard to believe.\n\nSo I guess there could be evidence, but I don’t know what would really convince me unless the comments stopped too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be wrong about certain drivers reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Possible… I mean, I guess anything is possible. People say that to me a lot, like “maybe you’re reading into it,” and I try to sit with that.\n\nBut then I come back to the same thing: dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer system, and then there are those little comments on the radio. Like someone says something small, but it fits too closely with where I was or what changed. And certain drivers… they just know things.\n\nIt reminds me of when my aunt used to say if two people know a secret, it’s not really a secret anymore—sorry, I know that’s kind of off. I just mean information gets around. So maybe I could be wrong in some tiny piece of it, but I don’t think I’m wrong that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: If dispatch and compliance showed you proof that no drivers are reporting on you, would you believe them?\n\nPatient: I’d want to believe them, I really would. If they sat me down and showed me the computer system, like who can see the route changes and what gets logged, maybe I’d feel calmer for a minute.\n\nBut I don’t know if proof from dispatch and compliance would be enough, because they’re part of it, or at least they’re the ones with the system. And then I’d get back in the truck and hear some little comment on the radio that lines up too well with where I was, and it would start all over again.\n\nIt’s like when someone shows you a receipt and says, “See, I paid,” but then the numbers don’t look right—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. I mean… I might listen to them, but I don’t think I’d fully believe it unless the radio comments stopped and certain drivers stopped seeming like they already know things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people reporting on you are trying to harm you or your job?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the exact word… like, I don’t think they’re all sitting there saying, “Let’s ruin her,” but it feels like it could affect my job, yeah. Because if certain drivers are reporting on my route changes, and dispatch and compliance are watching it through the computer system, then it’s not just normal oversight anymore.\n\nIt’s those little radio comments that make it feel pointed. Like they’ll say something that matches where I was or what got changed, and then I think, okay, someone is making sure this gets noticed. And once something gets noticed enough, it can turn into a write-up or people thinking I’m not doing things right.\n\nMy cousin had this thing at her warehouse where people kept “just mentioning” stuff to supervisors, and then suddenly she was the problem—sorry, that’s not exactly the same. I just mean… I don’t know if they want to hurt me personally, but I do think it could hurt my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that certain drivers are reporting on you is something that you might be imagining?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that, and I guess I know what you’re getting at. Like, am I filling in the blanks myself. But it doesn’t feel imagined when it keeps happening.\n\nI’m here because dispatch and compliance can track the route changes through the computer system, and then I hear these little comments on the radio that line up with it. And certain drivers—some of them just seem to know before they should. That’s the part I can’t shake.\n\nLike when you’re in a break room and everyone gets quiet right after you walk in—sorry, that’s not the same thing, I know. I just mean there’s a feeling when people are talking around something. So maybe my mind is… trying to make sense of it, but I don’t think I’m just imagining that certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief about certain drivers reporting on you could be a mistaken idea that is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could be mistaken, in the way anybody can misread something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. But that’s hard for me to accept because I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then these little comments come over the radio that line up with it.\n\nPeople say, “That’s just normal oversight,” and I get that trucking has systems and logs and all that.
